# Say what you want to see

Speak to a CT scan and it renders what you asked for — "show only the
skeleton," "a bit more kidneys." A rule parser or a local LLM turns the words
into an instruction, a policy trained with reinforcement learning proposes the
transfer function in one forward pass, planning through its own learned model
refines it, and a short search polishes it.

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/screenshots/ui.png" width="85%" alt="The viewer mid-session: a render, the instruction history with each command's per-class visibility delta, and the histogram/transfer-function curve panel" />
</p>

This is a master's thesis: can a transfer function be learned with RL well
enough to follow instructions on a patient it has never seen? On six held-out
patients the policy alone gets about half-way to the goal of a typical
instruction in one step, 0.64 after planning (no renders), 0.77 with 30
renders of search on top -- as good as 200 renders of search from scratch
(0.70) -- and 0.91 with 200.

## Try it

```bash
python server.py      # http://127.0.0.1:8000
```

Type or hold Space to talk. The viewer opens on a transfer function with the
organs in view; every command is answered by the policy, refined by planning
through its learned model (~0.2 s), then polished by a 200-evaluation search
(~5 s). Exact application of the parsed command and plain search are
one click away. **compare** answers one instruction four ways from the same
start — applied directly, hill-climbed at 10 and 200 evaluations, and by the
bare policy — with attainment and cost side by side; **sweep 20** does the
same over twenty sampled instructions.

| env var | default | |
|---|---|---|
| `POLICY_PATH` | `out/rl_mb_v3c/clb_r3_seed1/checkpoint.pt` | policy checkpoint (must match the task version) |
| `POLICY_PLAN` | `1` | `0` skips planning through the learned model |
| `POLICY_REFINE_EVALUATIONS` | `200` | search on top of the policy; `30` ≈ 0.77 in ~0.7 s, `0` shows the policy (+ planning) alone |
| `POLICY_STEPS` | `1` | times the policy acts per command (re-rendering in between) |
| `TASK_VERSION` | `v3` | `v2` (with `out/rl_mb_v2/clb_r3_seed1/checkpoint.pt`) or `v1` restores an earlier task |

### In a VR headset

```bash
python server.py --host 0.0.0.0 --ssl-certfile certs/lan.pem --ssl-keyfile certs/lan-key.pem
# headset browser: https://<mac-lan-ip>:8000/   (certificate from mkcert, must cover that IP)
```

A standalone headset (tested: Vive Focus 3) gets a custom WebGL2 ray marcher
instead of vtk.js: foveated step size, a longer stride through transparent
samples, brick skipping when the TF leaves bricks sparse, and coarser steps
while the volume turns -- 57-58 fps head-on and 84-88 fps while rotating on a
523-slice body scan, at the same resolution. Hold the trigger to talk; the
thumbstick turns the volume, a click resets it and brings it back in front of
you; the panel above it lists what each command changed, as the chat does.
URL flags (`renderer`, `down`, `xrscale`, `fovstep`, ...) and the debugging
tools are in `tools/headset/README.md`; measurements in
`docs/experiments/2026-10-19-vr-lab-session.md` and
`docs/experiments/2026-10-15-vr-lab-session.md`.

## Why this is hard

"More kidneys" has no closed-form answer — it depends on what sits in front
of the kidneys on that particular scan and what else lights up when their
peak moves. Instructions are scored on what the render actually shows:
`visibility.py` estimates each labelled class's visible share of the image
(14 ms against 50–210 ms for a real render), and `goals.py` turns an
instruction into target changes per class and measures **attainment** — 1
means the goal was reached, 0 means no progress, negative means worse than
not touching anything; changing an unmentioned class costs.

Eight classes, all from TotalSegmentator labels: skeleton, lungs, heart,
vessels, liver, kidneys, spleen, soft tissue. The transfer function has one
peak per class, and a labelled voxel is shaded by its own class's peak only
(`transfer_mode="label_scoped"`), so organs that share a Hounsfield range —
soft 40, kidneys 45, heart 50, spleen 55, liver 65 HU — no longer light each
other up. Unlabelled tissue (skin, fat, muscle) still sees peaks by HU.

## The task: v1, v2, v3

On the original task (v1) about 30 % of instructions could not be improved by
any method, even a 1000-evaluation search. They were ill-posed, not hard:
"less lungs" when the lungs were already invisible, "more spleen" when the
spleen sat behind the skin shell, so revealing it was charged as overshoot
and fading the shell as drift. Task v2 fixes the definition, not the learner:

1. the viewer and training start on a TF with the organs in view;
2. instructions are well-posed (no "less X" for an invisible X);
3. a class hiding a target may fade without penalty (`goals.find_occluders`);
4. targets are clipped to what a class can physically reach.

That lifts what a 500-evaluation search can reach from 0.49 to 0.82. v1 stays
available (`task_version="v1"`); results on the two tasks are reported side
by side, never as one number improving on the other
(`docs/experiments/2026-10-17-task-v2.md`).

Task v3 is what the viewer runs. On v2 the unlabelled shell -- skin and fat lit
by the soft-tissue peak -- still covered half the image, so a "more liver"
scored as complete moved half a percent of it. v3 draws unlabelled tissue at
0.2 opacity, asks brightness only of classes that are visible, and lets
"show only" hide everything else completely, and a class's reachable ceiling
is probed at several peak heights (soft tissue is most visible at a low one).
Policy + 200 evaluations reaches the goal as often as on v2 (0.88-0.92) with
two to three times the on-screen change (median 2.5-3 percentage points of the
image against 1) (`docs/experiments/2026-10-19-task-v3.md`). Every evaluation also reports
**visible pp**, the percentage points of the image moved toward the goal.

## The policy

Model-based RL (`rl/model_based.py`). An ensemble of five MLPs learns what a
transfer-function change does to the measured visibility, from renders of
random changes on 90 training patients. The policy is trained by
backpropagating the exact goal distance through that learned model; Dyna
rounds render the policy's own proposals and refit the model where it was
wrong. A closed-loop fine-tune (`rl/closed_loop.py`) trains through two-step
rollouts, with every instruction kind drawn equally often. At use, the
policy's answer is refined by gradient descent through its own learned model
(`rl.model_based.plan`, no renders, ~0.2 s). Model-free SAC on the same task
stayed at a median of 0.00-0.09.

Task v3, 300 episodes per split, median attainment (median visible pp);
policy rows are three training seeds:

| method | evaluations | val | **test** (run once) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **policy + planning + search** (viewer default) | 200 | 0.89 / 0.90 / 0.90 (2.9-3.1) | **0.91 / 0.92 / 0.90 (2.5)** |
| **policy + planning + search** | 30 | 0.75 / 0.77 / 0.75 (2.7-3.0) | **0.77 / 0.78 / 0.77 (2.3-2.5)** |
| search (hill climb) | 200 | 0.75 (2.1) | 0.70 (2.0) |
| **policy + planning** | 0 | 0.62 / 0.63 / 0.63 (2.6-2.7) | **0.62 / 0.65 / 0.64 (2.3-2.5)** |
| **policy alone** | 0 | 0.50 / 0.49 / 0.49 (1.4-1.6) | **0.48 / 0.47 / 0.47 (1.2-1.5)** |

Planning beats the policy alone, and planning + 30 beats policy + 30, on every
seed and split (paired Wilcoxon, p ≤ 3e-5); planning + 30 is level with the
200-evaluation search from scratch (p = 0.14-0.9).

Task v2, 300 episodes per split, the same episodes for every method, median
attainment; policy rows are three training seeds:

| method | evaluations | val | **test** (run once) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **policy + search** (viewer default) | 200 | 0.89 / 0.89 / 0.89 | **0.90 / 0.91 / 0.90** |
| **policy + search** | 100 | 0.85 / 0.87 / 0.85 | **0.86 / 0.86 / 0.85** |
| **policy + search** | 30 | 0.70 / 0.72 / 0.71 | **0.71 / 0.70 / 0.70** |
| search (hill climb) | 200 | 0.66 | 0.65 |
| **policy alone** | 0 | 0.50 / 0.54 / 0.50 | **0.49 / 0.51 / 0.50** |
| search | 100 | 0.48 | 0.49 |
| search | 30 | 0.13 | 0.14 |
| search | 10 | 0.04 | 0.05 |
| rule executor | 0 | 0.03 | 0.01 |

On v2:

- The policy alone beats every baseline except the 200-evaluation search
  (paired Wilcoxon p < 1e-15) and generalises: val 0.51, test 0.50.
- As a starting point it beats search from scratch at every budget
  (p < 1e-15). Policy + 30 evaluations is level with a 200-evaluation search;
  with 100 it is clearly ahead.

By kind (v2, test, seed 1):

| kind | policy | + 30 | + 200 | search 200 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| absolute ("high opacity vessels") | 0.62 | 0.78 | 0.96 | 0.71 |
| relative ("more liver") | 0.53 | 0.76 | 0.97 | 0.74 |
| compound ("more bone, less lungs") | 0.43 | 0.63 | 0.82 | 0.65 |
| show only | 0.40 | 0.53 | 0.70 | 0.35 |
| brightness | 0.79 | 0.89 | 0.93 | 0.99 |

On v1 the same learner reaches 0.23, against at most 0.09 for SAC
(`docs/experiments/2026-10-16-overnight-results.md`).

## What didn't work

- **Model-free SAC** (experiments E1–E9) — a median of at most 0.09 however the
  reward, pretraining or show-only weighting was set. Its critic had to learn
  instruction × rendering physics from one scalar.
- **Behaviour cloning on search results** — negative even as the imitation
  loss improved: averaging several good answers gives a bad one.
- **A ten-step formulation**, nudging parameters over ten steps — never
  learned. Letting the trained policy act twice with a re-render in between
  is no better than once either (p = 0.37).
- **More Dyna rounds** — the fourth added nothing.
- **Intensity-band tissue labels** ("fat", "spongy") — needed real anatomy
  labels. **Measuring visibility by deleting voxels** opens a hole that
  reveals what is behind; blacking out colour, not opacity, is correct.

## Learning from people

A search needs tens to hundreds of renders per instruction, which no one will
rate; a policy answers in one, which makes learning from human preference
feasible. `/collect` shows two candidate renders per instruction, judged blind
(A, B, equal, skip); `tools.merge_preferences` merges several raters' files
and reports their agreement — the ceiling for any reward model trained on
them. Judgments collected before a rendering fix are kept apart as a pilot.

Since 2026-10-19 `/collect` renders the way the viewer does (task v3,
label-scoped, faded shell; six views, cropped rather than squeezed) and pairs
two of the conditions the thesis compares: the policy alone, policy +
planning, + 30 or + 200 renders of search, 200 renders of search from the
start, and the rule executor. Rows go to `out/vis_preferences_v3.jsonl` with
each candidate's source and attainment, so agreement with the objective can be
measured per condition. An item takes ~5–9 s to prepare; the 40 shared anchor
items are cached in `out/cache/anchor_items.json` (built on first use, ~minutes).

## Data

TotalSegmentator CT (CC-BY-4.0): 20 train / 4 val / 6 test patients, plus 70
more training patients (`train_extra`) for the model-based learner, and four
Slicer CTs as an out-of-source set. Val and test are the same for every
result.

```bash
curl -L -o data/Totalsegmentator_dataset_small_v201.zip \
  "https://zenodo.org/records/10047263/files/Totalsegmentator_dataset_small_v201.zip?download=1"
python -m tools.select_totalseg
python -m tools.extend_totalseg
python -m tools.build_visibility_cache
python -m tools.build_visibility_cache --splits train_extra
```

## Reproduce the policy

```bash
# task v2 shown; task v3 is the same with --task-version v3
# (docs/experiments/2026-10-19-scripts/pipeline_v3.sh)
B="--splits train,train_extra --task-version v2"
python -m rl.model_based collect --out out/rl_mb_v2/data_r0b.npz --starts 3000 --seed-base 7000000 $B
python -m rl.model_based train --data out/rl_mb_v2/data_r0b.npz --out out/rl_mb_v2/r0b_seed0 --task-version v2
# Dyna rounds: render the latest policy's proposals, retrain on everything so far
python -m rl.model_based collect --out out/rl_mb_v2/data_r1.npz --starts 1500 --seed-base 8000000 \
    --policy out/rl_mb_v2/r0b_seed0/checkpoint.pt $B
# ... data_r2 (seed base 9000000, policy r1_seed2), data_r3 (4000000, policy cl2_r2_seed2), then per seed:
D=out/rl_mb_v2/data_r0b.npz,out/rl_mb_v2/data_r1.npz,out/rl_mb_v2/data_r2.npz,out/rl_mb_v2/data_r3.npz
python -m rl.model_based train --data $D --out out/rl_mb_v2/r3_seed1 --seed 1 --task-version v2
python -m rl.closed_loop --init out/rl_mb_v2/r3_seed1/checkpoint.pt --data $D \
    --out out/rl_mb_v2/clb_r3_seed1 --seed 1 --balance-kinds --task-version v2

# official scores (300 fixed episodes; --refine N adds policy + N-evaluation search)
python -m rl.vis_eval --policy out/rl_mb_v2/clb_r3_seed1/checkpoint.pt --split val
```

The per-round seeds, policies and logs are in
`docs/experiments/2026-10-17-task-v2.md`; the probes behind its tables are in
`docs/experiments/2026-10-17-scripts/`.

## Setup

```bash
python3 -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
python -m pytest -q -m "not slow"
```

Run from the repository root — paths like `out/` and `static/` are relative.
Everything runs on CPU. Voice commands go through one vocabulary,
`data/lexicon.json`: Whisper gets it as a prompt, known transcription errors
are normalized ("Darkin' Skeleton" → "darken skeleton"), the rule parser
answers when it accounts for every word, and only the rest goes to the local
LLM (Ollama, `ollama serve && ollama pull qwen2.5:7b`; the server checks and
preloads it at start, and falls back to the rule parser without it). New
words go into `data/lexicon.json`. Parsers are scored by `eval_parsers.py`
(`--set realistic` for the spoken-style set; see
`docs/experiments/2026-10-18-instruction-parser.md` and the 10-21 lexicon
spec). In policy mode the viewer shows a fast answer at once and replaces it
with the refined one a few seconds later (`POLICY_TWO_STAGE`,
`docs/experiments/2026-10-23-keep-repair-and-refine-budget.md`).

## Layout

| Path | What's there |
|---|---|
| `server.py`, `static/` | web UI, voice, WebXR viewer, compare panel, preference collection |
| `commands.py`, `asr.py`, `eval_parsers.py` | instruction parsing, speech, parser evaluation |
| `transfer.py`, `anatomy.py`, `anatomy_layers.py`, `render.py` | transfer functions, classes, rendering |
| `visibility.py`, `goals.py` | what a render shows; instructions, task v1/v2/v3, attainment, visible pp |
| `rl/model_based.py`, `rl/closed_loop.py` | the policy: dynamics ensemble, training, Dyna, closed-loop fine-tune, planning |
| `rl/vis_eval.py`, `rl/baselines.py` | official evaluation, search and rule baselines |
| `rl/oneshot_env.py`, `rl/oneshot_train.py`, `rl/pretrain.py` | the environment; SAC experiments |
| `policy.py` | checkpoint loading for the viewer |
| `static/vr_renderer.js`, `static/xr.js`, `static/xr_logic.js` | VR: ray marcher, WebXR session and panel, pure logic (tested by `tests/js/`) |
| `datasets.py`, `totalseg.py`, `tools/` | volumes, splits, labels, caches; `tools/headset/` drives the headset over adb |
| `docs/experiments/` | one dated write-up per experiment, with scripts |

### anatomy.py
```
"""Canonical anatomy classes and structure-to-class registry."""

CANONICAL_CLASSES = (
    "skeleton",
    "lungs",
    "heart",
    "vessels",
    "liver",
    "kidneys",
    "spleen",
    "soft",
)
CLASS_NAMES = CANONICAL_CLASSES
MEASURED_CLASSES = CANONICAL_CLASSES + ("other",)
LAYOUT_VERSION = "anatomy-v2"
CLASS_LAYOUT_VERSION = LAYOUT_VERSION
PROMOTED_ORGAN_CLASSES = ("liver", "kidneys", "spleen", "heart")
CANONICAL_PEAK_ORDER = (
    "lungs",
    "soft",
    "liver",
    "kidneys",
    "spleen",
    "heart",
    "vessels",
    "skeleton",
)

STRUCTURE_MAPPINGS = {
    "liver": "liver",
    "kidney_left": "kidneys",
    "kidney_right": "kidneys",
    "spleen": "spleen",
    "heart": "heart",
}

STRUCTURE_RULES = {
    "skeleton": (
        "rib_",
        "vertebrae_",
        "hip_",
        "femur_",
        "humerus_",
        "scapula_",
        "clavicula_",
        "sacrum",
        "sternum",
        "skull",
        "costal_cartilages",
        "patella",
        "tibia",
        "fibula",
        "carpal",
        "metacarpal",
        "phalanges",
        "tarsal",
        "metatarsal",
    ),
    "lungs": ("lung_",),
    "heart": ("heart", "atrial_appendage"),
    "liver": ("liver",),
    "kidneys": ("kidney_",),
    "spleen": ("spleen",),
    "vessels": (
        "vessel_",
        "aorta",
        "brachiocephalic_",
        "common_carotid_",
        "subclavian_",
        "pulmonary_",
        "vena_cava",
        "portal_vein",
        "iliac_artery",
        "iliac_vena",
        "superior_vena_cava",
        "inferior_vena_cava",
    ),
    "soft": (
        "stomach",
        "pancreas",
        "gallbladder",
        "colon",
        "small_bowel",
        "duodenum",
        "esophagus",
        "urinary_bladder",
        "prostate",
        "adrenal_gland_",
        "thyroid_gland",
        "brain",
        "spinal_cord",
        "trachea",
        "autochthon_",
        "gluteus_",
        "iliopsoas_",
    ),
}

STRUCTURE_PREFIXES = STRUCTURE_RULES


def class_for_structure(structure: str) -> str | None:
    """Return canonical class for a TotalSegmentator structure name."""
    if structure in STRUCTURE_MAPPINGS:
        return STRUCTURE_MAPPINGS[structure]
    for anatomy_class, prefixes in STRUCTURE_RULES.items():
        if structure.startswith(prefixes):
            return anatomy_class
    return None
```

### anatomy_layers.py
```
"""Versioned anatomical layer settings and validation."""

from __future__ import annotations

import copy
import math
from numbers import Real
from typing import Any, Mapping

import anatomy


LAYER_LAYOUT_VERSION = "anatomy-layers-v1"
DEFAULT_LAYERS = {
    "skeleton": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.95, 0.95, 0.90]},
    "lungs": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.55, 0.70, 0.95]},
    "heart": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.85, 0.25, 0.25]},
    "vessels": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.90, 0.45, 0.40]},
    "liver": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.75, 0.45, 0.25]},
    "kidneys": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.70, 0.40, 0.35]},
    "spleen": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.55, 0.30, 0.45]},
    "soft": {"opacity": 1.0, "rgb": [0.85, 0.35, 0.35]},
}


def default_layers() -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
    """Return an independent copy of canonical layer defaults."""
    return copy.deepcopy(DEFAULT_LAYERS)


def _bounded_number(value: Any, field: str) -> float:
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, Real):
        raise ValueError(f"{field} must be numeric")
    value = float(value)
    if not math.isfinite(value) or not 0 <= value <= 1:
        raise ValueError(f"{field} must be finite and in [0, 1]")
    return value


def _require_string_keys(value: Mapping[Any, Any], field: str) -> None:
    if any(not isinstance(key, str) for key in value):
        raise ValueError(f"{field} keys must be strings")


def normalize_layers(
    value: Mapping[str, Any], available_classes: set[str] | None = None
) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
    """Validate layer overrides and return all canonical classes with defaults."""
    if not isinstance(value, Mapping):
        raise ValueError("anatomy_layers must be an object")
    _require_string_keys(value, "anatomy_layers")
    unknown = set(value) - set(anatomy.CANONICAL_CLASSES)
    if unknown:
        raise ValueError(f"anatomy_layers has unknown class: {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}")
    if available_classes is not None:
        available = set(available_classes)
        if any(not isinstance(class_name, str) for class_name in available):
            raise ValueError("available_classes keys must be strings")
        unknown_available = available - set(anatomy.CANONICAL_CLASSES)
        if unknown_available:
            raise ValueError(
                "available_classes has unknown class: "
                + ", ".join(sorted(unknown_available))
            )
        unsupported = set(value) - available
        if unsupported:
            raise ValueError(
                "anatomy_layers contains unsupported class: "
                + ", ".join(sorted(unsupported))
            )

    result = default_layers()
    for class_name, settings in value.items():
        if not isinstance(settings, Mapping):
            raise ValueError(f"anatomy_layers.{class_name} must be an object")
        _require_string_keys(settings, f"anatomy_layers.{class_name}")
        unknown_settings = set(settings) - {"opacity", "rgb"}
        if unknown_settings:
            raise ValueError(
                f"anatomy_layers.{class_name} has unknown fields: "
                + ", ".join(sorted(unknown_settings))
            )
        layer = result[class_name]
        if "opacity" in settings:
            layer["opacity"] = _bounded_number(
                settings["opacity"], f"anatomy_layers.{class_name}.opacity"
            )
        if "rgb" in settings:
            rgb = settings["rgb"]
            if not isinstance(rgb, (list, tuple)) or len(rgb) != 3:
                raise ValueError(f"anatomy_layers.{class_name}.rgb must contain 3 values")
            layer["rgb"] = [
                _bounded_number(component, f"anatomy_layers.{class_name}.rgb[{index}]")
                for index, component in enumerate(rgb)
            ]
    return result
```

### asr.py
```
"""Speech recognition for voice commands: audio file -> text.

Audio is captured in the browser and POSTed to `/api/transcribe`. Two
backends: mlx-whisper (Apple GPU) and faster-whisper (CPU, int8); ASR_BACKEND
picks one, otherwise mlx-whisper when installed. ASR_MODEL picks the size.
"""
import os
import re

_MODEL_CACHE = {}
SAMPLE_RATE = 16000
AUDIO_DIR = "out/audio"
ASR_BACKEND = os.environ.get("ASR_BACKEND")  # "mlx" | "faster-whisper" | None
ASR_MODEL = os.environ.get("ASR_MODEL", "small")
MLX_REPOS = {"small": "mlx-community/whisper-small-mlx", "turbo": "mlx-community/whisper-large-v3-turbo"}
FASTER_WHISPER_NAMES = {"small": "small", "turbo": "large-v3-turbo"}

# What Whisper says on silence or noise (2026-10-21 VR session: "You" eleven
# times, invented sentences twice). Commands are short.
HALLUCINATIONS = {"you", "thank you", "thanks", "thank you for watching", "thanks for watching", "bye",
                  "okay", "ok", "so", "um", "uh"}
MAX_COMMAND_WORDS = 20


def is_hallucination(text: str) -> bool:
    t = re.sub(r"[^a-z' ]", "", text.lower()).strip()
    return not t or t in HALLUCINATIONS or len(t.split()) > MAX_COMMAND_WORDS


def _default_backend() -> str:
    if ASR_BACKEND:
        return ASR_BACKEND
    try:
        import mlx_whisper  # noqa: F401  # pyright: ignore[reportMissingImports]
        return "mlx"
    except ImportError:
        return "faster-whisper"


def _load_model(model_size: str, backend: str):
    key = (backend, model_size)
    if key in _MODEL_CACHE:
        return _MODEL_CACHE[key]
    if backend == "mlx":
        _MODEL_CACHE[key] = ("mlx", MLX_REPOS[model_size])
        print(f"[asr] using mlx-whisper ({MLX_REPOS[model_size]})")
    else:
        from faster_whisper import WhisperModel
        model = WhisperModel(FASTER_WHISPER_NAMES[model_size], device="cpu", compute_type="int8")
        _MODEL_CACHE[key] = ("faster-whisper", model)
        print(f"[asr] using faster-whisper ({FASTER_WHISPER_NAMES[model_size]}, cpu, int8)")
    return _MODEL_CACHE[key]


def _transcribe_path(path: str, lang: str, model_size: str = ASR_MODEL, initial_prompt: str = None,
                     backend: str = None) -> str:
    kind, model = _load_model(model_size, backend or _default_backend())
    if kind == "mlx":
        import mlx_whisper  # pyright: ignore[reportMissingImports]
        result = mlx_whisper.transcribe(path, path_or_hf_repo=model, language=lang,
                                        initial_prompt=initial_prompt, condition_on_previous_text=False)
        return result["text"].strip()
    segments, _ = model.transcribe(path, language=lang, initial_prompt=initial_prompt, vad_filter=True,
                                   condition_on_previous_text=False)
    return " ".join(seg.text.strip() for seg in segments).strip()
```

### camera.py
```
"""Camera state (azimuth/elevation/zoom) and relative camera commands."""
import numpy as np

DEFAULT_CAMERA = {"azimuth": 30.0, "elevation": 20.0, "zoom": 1.0}  # copy before use, e.g. dict(DEFAULT_CAMERA) -- never hold a live reference to this dict

ELEVATION_RANGE = (-85.0, 85.0)
ZOOM_RANGE = (0.3, 4.0)

ROTATE_STEP_DEGREES = {"slightly": 15.0, "moderately": 30.0, "strongly": 60.0}
ZOOM_STEP_FACTOR = {"slightly": 1.15, "moderately": 1.35, "strongly": 1.7}


def apply_camera_command(cam_cmd: dict, camera: dict) -> dict:
    """Apply one relative camera adjustment, returning a new camera dict.

    cam_cmd: {"action": "rotate"|"tilt"|"zoom",
              "direction": "left"|"right" (rotate) | "up"|"down" (tilt) | "in"|"out" (zoom),
              "strength": "slightly"|"moderately"|"strongly"}

    Azimuth wraps (mod 360) since it has no natural bound. Elevation and
    zoom clamp instead, since both have real physical limits -- clamping
    elevation specifically avoids the camera flipping through the poles.
    Does not mutate the input `camera` dict.
    """
    camera = dict(camera)
    action = cam_cmd["action"]
    direction = cam_cmd["direction"]
    strength = cam_cmd.get("strength") or "moderately"

    if action == "rotate":
        delta = ROTATE_STEP_DEGREES[strength]
        sign = 1.0 if direction == "right" else -1.0
        camera["azimuth"] = (camera["azimuth"] + sign * delta) % 360.0
    elif action == "tilt":
        delta = ROTATE_STEP_DEGREES[strength]
        sign = 1.0 if direction == "up" else -1.0
        camera["elevation"] = float(np.clip(camera["elevation"] + sign * delta, *ELEVATION_RANGE))
    elif action == "zoom":
        factor = ZOOM_STEP_FACTOR[strength]
        if direction == "in":
            camera["zoom"] = float(np.clip(camera["zoom"] * factor, *ZOOM_RANGE))
        else:
            camera["zoom"] = float(np.clip(camera["zoom"] / factor, *ZOOM_RANGE))
    return camera
```

### collect.py
```
"""FastAPI router for the preference-collection page (`/collect`).

Route handlers are `async def`, for the same reason `server.py`'s are (see
its module docstring, and `tests/test_server.py`'s): `next_route` renders
images (`collect_images.view_grid`, through `Collector.image_fn`), and VTK's
Cocoa render window can only be created on the true process main thread on
macOS. Keeping the handlers `async def` keeps every render call on the
event-loop thread, which `uvicorn.run()` in `server.py`'s `__main__` drives
from the process main thread. Tests call `Collector` methods and the route
coroutines directly (`asyncio.run(...)`) rather than through `TestClient`,
which runs the app on a background thread and would violate that constraint.

`Collector` holds all item-generation, pending-item and repeat-scheduling
state, independent of FastAPI, so it is directly unit-testable and every
external seam (volume list, model lookup, image rendering, dataset version,
item sampling, the policy) is injectable.
"""
import collections
import datetime
import os
import threading
import uuid

import numpy as np
from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException
from fastapi.responses import FileResponse
from pydantic import BaseModel

import goals
import visibility
from collect_images import grid_data_url
from datasets import _dataset_version, volumes_for_split
from evaluate import jsonl_append
import policy as policy_module
from rl.candidates import anchor_items, sample_item
from rl.oneshot_env import load_policy_metadata, resolve_policy_metadata

# Judgments on task-v3 renders (label-scoped, faded shell) with the
# model-based candidates (rl.candidates.TASK_SOURCES), from 2026-10-19 on. The
# 59 earlier rows in out/vis_preferences.jsonl compare v1-era renders and
# SAC-era sources -- a pilot, kept in its own file and never mixed in.
PREF_PATH = "out/vis_preferences_v3.jsonl"
_WRITE_LOCK = threading.Lock()

VALID_CHOICES = ("a", "b", "equal", "skip")
VALID_ROLES = ("radiologist", "clinician", "researcher", "other")
REPEAT_RATE = 0.10          # share of items that are a repeat of an earlier one
REPEAT_MIN_GAP = 20         # a repeat is shown only >= this many items after the original
ANCHOR_RATE = 0.20          # share of items drawn from the shared anchor pool (roughly one in five)

# Re-exported so existing callers and tests keep working; `policy.py` owns the
# value. These were two separate constants until they drifted apart -- see that
# module's docstring.
POLICY_PATH = policy_module.POLICY_PATH
_load_policy = policy_module.load_policy


def _policy_metadata():
    """The shipped checkpoint's contract (task version, action mode), or the
    v1 default when no checkpoint exists -- `rl.candidates.sample_item` picks
    the task-v2/v3 item path from it."""
    if os.path.exists(POLICY_PATH):
        return load_policy_metadata(POLICY_PATH)
    return resolve_policy_metadata()


def _to_displayed(raw: dict) -> dict:
    """`rl.candidates.sample_item`'s output, narrowed to the fields the
    displayed item needs (drops `"volume"`'s duplication handled elsewhere,
    keeps everything `_swap_sides`/`_response`/the stored row use)."""
    return {
        "volume": raw["volume"],
        "start_params": raw["start_params"],
        "instruction": raw["instruction"],
        "a": raw["a"],
        "b": raw["b"],
        "objective_choice": raw["objective_choice"],
        "near_duplicate": raw["near_duplicate"],
        "features": raw["features"],
    }


def _swap_sides(item: dict) -> dict:
    """The same item with displayed `a`/`b` exchanged -- used both to
    randomize which side a freshly sampled candidate lands on, and to show a
    repeat with sides swapped from the original."""
    features = {**item["features"], "a": item["features"]["b"], "b": item["features"]["a"]}
    if "attainment" in features:
        features["attainment"] = {"a": features["attainment"]["b"], "b": features["attainment"]["a"]}
    return {
        **item,
        "a": item["b"],
        "b": item["a"],
        "objective_choice": {"a": "b", "b": "a"}[item["objective_choice"]],
        "features": features,
    }


def _instruction_json(instruction: dict) -> dict:
    return {
        "kind": instruction["kind"],
        "text": None if instruction["text"] is None else str(instruction["text"]),
        "targets": instruction["targets"],
        "goal": np.asarray(instruction["goal"], dtype=np.float64).tolist(),
        **({"weights": {c: float(w) for c, w in instruction["weights"].items()}}
           if instruction.get("weights") else {}),
    }


class Collector:
    """Item generation, pending-item state, repeat scheduling and the
    append-only judgment log for `/collect`.

    Every external seam is injectable so tests never touch VTK, real
    datasets or a real policy checkpoint: `model_for_volume` (default
    `visibility.for_volume`), `image_fn` (default `collect_images.
    grid_data_url`), `dataset_version_fn` (default `datasets._dataset_
    version`), `sample_item_fn` (default `rl.candidates.sample_item`),
    `anchor_items_fn` (default `rl.candidates.anchor_items`),
    `policy_provider` (default `_load_policy`, called -- lazily -- on every
    sampled item) and `policy_metadata_fn` (default `_policy_metadata`).
    A checkpoint trained on task v2/v3 switches this process to that task
    (`goals.use_task_version`), so the renders show what the policy saw.
    """

    def __init__(self, pref_path: str = PREF_PATH, volumes=None, rng: np.random.Generator = None,
                 policy_provider=None, model_for_volume=visibility.for_volume,
                 image_fn=grid_data_url, dataset_version_fn=_dataset_version,
                 sample_item_fn=sample_item, anchor_items_fn=anchor_items, policy_metadata_fn=None):
        self.pref_path = pref_path
        self._volumes = list(volumes) if volumes is not None else None
        self.rng = rng if rng is not None else np.random.default_rng()
        self.policy_provider = policy_provider or _load_policy
        self.model_for_volume = model_for_volume
        self.image_fn = image_fn
        self.dataset_version_fn = dataset_version_fn
        self.sample_item_fn = sample_item_fn
        self.anchor_items_fn = anchor_items_fn
        self.policy_metadata_fn = policy_metadata_fn or (lambda: resolve_policy_metadata())
        self._policy_metadata = None

        self._model_cache = {}
        self._items = {}                                  # pair_id -> displayed item, kept for repeats
        self._pending = {}                                 # pair_id -> rater_id, not yet judged
        self._repeat_of = {}                                # pair_id -> original pair_id or None
        self._anchor_id = {}                                # pair_id -> anchor pool index or None
        self._rater_history = collections.defaultdict(list)  # rater_id -> judged pair_id, in order
        self._rater_meta = {}                               # rater_id -> {"role", "experience"}
        self._anchor_pool = None                            # lazily generated, then cached for the process
        self._rater_anchor_seen = collections.defaultdict(set)  # rater_id -> anchor indices already served

    def _get_volumes(self) -> list:
        # Resolved lazily (not at construction) so importing this module, and
        # building the default module-level Collector, never depends on the
        # TotalSegmentator data being present on disk.
        if self._volumes is None:
            self._volumes = volumes_for_split("train") + volumes_for_split("val")
        return self._volumes

    def _get_model(self, volume: str):
        model = self._model_cache.get(volume)
        if model is None:
            model = self.model_for_volume(volume)
            self._model_cache[volume] = model
        return model

    def _get_policy_metadata(self) -> dict:
        if self._policy_metadata is None:
            self._policy_metadata = self.policy_metadata_fn()
            task_version = self._policy_metadata.get("task_version", "v1")
            if task_version != "v1":
                goals.use_task_version(task_version)
        return self._policy_metadata

    def _sample_kwargs(self) -> dict:
        # Only a task checkpoint adds the kwarg, so injected sample_item stubs
        # with the original signature keep working.
        metadata = self._get_policy_metadata()
        return {} if metadata.get("task_version", "v1") == "v1" else {"policy_metadata": metadata}

    def _should_repeat(self, rater_id: str) -> bool:
        history = self._rater_history[rater_id]
        return len(history) >= REPEAT_MIN_GAP and self.rng.random() < REPEAT_RATE

    def _get_anchor_pool(self) -> list:
        # Generated at most once per process (the module-level `collector`
        # lives for the life of the server) -- `anchor_items_fn` itself
        # caches to disk (see `rl.candidates.anchor_items`), so a restart is
        # cheap too.
        if self._anchor_pool is None:
            # A task checkpoint's anchors include its own candidates, so the
            # pool is tied to that checkpoint (`policy_id`).
            kwargs = self._sample_kwargs()
            if kwargs:
                kwargs.update(policy=self.policy_provider(), policy_id=POLICY_PATH)
            self._anchor_pool = self.anchor_items_fn(volumes=self._get_volumes(),
                                                       model_for_volume=self.model_for_volume, **kwargs)
        return self._anchor_pool

    def _next_anchor_index(self, rater_id: str):
        """The lowest anchor-pool index `rater_id` has not yet been served,
        in the same deterministic order (0, 1, 2, ...) for every rater --
        or `None` once the pool is exhausted for them."""
        pool = self._get_anchor_pool()
        seen = self._rater_anchor_seen[rater_id]
        for i in range(len(pool)):
            if i not in seen:
                return i
        return None

    def _serve(self, rater_id: str) -> tuple:
        """Generate (or repeat, or draw from the anchor pool) one item for
        `rater_id`, register it as pending judgment, and return `(pair_id,
        item, repeat_of, anchor_id)`."""
        repeat_of = None
        anchor_id = None
        if self._should_repeat(rater_id):
            history = self._rater_history[rater_id]
            eligible = history[:len(history) - REPEAT_MIN_GAP + 1]
            repeat_of = str(self.rng.choice(eligible))
            item = _swap_sides(self._items[repeat_of])
        elif (candidate := self._next_anchor_index(rater_id)) is not None and self.rng.random() < ANCHOR_RATE:
            anchor_id = candidate
            self._rater_anchor_seen[rater_id].add(anchor_id)
            item = _to_displayed(self._get_anchor_pool()[anchor_id])
            if self.rng.random() < 0.5:
                item = _swap_sides(item)
        else:
            volume = str(self.rng.choice(self._get_volumes()))
            model = self._get_model(volume)
            raw = self.sample_item_fn(volume, model, self.rng, policy=self.policy_provider(),
                                      **self._sample_kwargs())
            item = _to_displayed(raw)
            if self.rng.random() < 0.5:
                item = _swap_sides(item)

        pair_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
        self._items[pair_id] = item
        self._pending[pair_id] = rater_id
        self._repeat_of[pair_id] = repeat_of
        self._anchor_id[pair_id] = anchor_id
        return pair_id, item, repeat_of, anchor_id

    def _response(self, pair_id: str, item: dict, repeat_of) -> dict:
        volume = item["volume"]
        return {
            "pair_id": pair_id,
            "text": None if item["instruction"]["text"] is None else str(item["instruction"]["text"]),
            "kind": item["instruction"]["kind"],
            "volume": volume,
            "a_image": self.image_fn(volume, item["a"]["params"]),
            "b_image": self.image_fn(volume, item["b"]["params"]),
            "start_image": self.image_fn(volume, item["start_params"]),
            "repeat_of": repeat_of,
        }

    def next_item(self, rater_id: str, rater_role: str = None, rater_experience: str = None) -> dict:
        """`rater_role`/`rater_experience` are asked once by the frontend
        (alongside the rater ID) and resent on every `/next` call, same as
        `rater_id` itself; when given here they (re)record this rater's
        metadata, used on every row they judge from now on (see `judge`).
        Omitting them (as `judge`'s own internal call does) keeps whatever
        was recorded earlier for this `rater_id`. Raises `ValueError` for a
        `rater_role` outside `VALID_ROLES`."""
        if rater_role is not None:
            if rater_role not in VALID_ROLES:
                raise ValueError(f"invalid role: {rater_role!r}, expected one of {VALID_ROLES}")
            self._rater_meta[rater_id] = {"role": rater_role, "experience": rater_experience}
        pair_id, item, repeat_of, _anchor_id = self._serve(rater_id)
        return self._response(pair_id, item, repeat_of)

    def judge(self, pair_id: str, choice: str, decision_ms) -> dict:
        """Append one row for `pair_id` and return the next item. Raises
        `ValueError` for an invalid `choice`, `KeyError` for an unknown or
        already-judged `pair_id`."""
        if choice not in VALID_CHOICES:
            raise ValueError(f"invalid choice: {choice!r}, expected one of {VALID_CHOICES}")
        if pair_id not in self._pending:
            raise KeyError(f"unknown pair_id: {pair_id!r}")

        rater_id = self._pending.pop(pair_id)
        item = self._items[pair_id]
        volume = item["volume"]
        rater_meta = self._rater_meta.get(rater_id, {"role": None, "experience": None})
        row = {
            "pair_id": pair_id,
            "timestamp": datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "rater_id": rater_id,                    # kept for backwards compatibility with earlier rows
            "rater": {"id": rater_id, "role": rater_meta["role"], "experience": rater_meta["experience"]},
            "volume": volume,
            "volume_version": self.dataset_version_fn(volume),
            "instruction": _instruction_json(item["instruction"]),
            "start_params": np.asarray(item["start_params"], dtype=np.float64).tolist(),
            "a": {"params": np.asarray(item["a"]["params"], dtype=np.float64).tolist(),
                  "source": item["a"]["source"]},
            "b": {"params": np.asarray(item["b"]["params"], dtype=np.float64).tolist(),
                  "source": item["b"]["source"]},
            "choice": choice,
            "objective_choice": item["objective_choice"],
            "near_duplicate": bool(item["near_duplicate"]),
            "features": item["features"],
            "decision_ms": decision_ms,
            "repeat_of": self._repeat_of.get(pair_id),
            "anchor_id": self._anchor_id.get(pair_id),
            "task_version": self._get_policy_metadata().get("task_version", "v1"),
            "policy_path": POLICY_PATH,
        }
        with _WRITE_LOCK:
            os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(self.pref_path) or ".", exist_ok=True)
            jsonl_append(self.pref_path, row)
        self._rater_history[rater_id].append(pair_id)

        return self.next_item(rater_id)


router = APIRouter()
collector = Collector(policy_metadata_fn=_policy_metadata)


class NextRequest(BaseModel):
    rater_id: str
    rater_role: str | None = None
    rater_experience: str | None = None


class JudgeRequest(BaseModel):
    pair_id: str
    choice: str
    decision_ms: float | None = None


@router.get("/collect")
async def collect_page():
    return FileResponse("static/collect.html")


@router.post("/api/collect/next")
async def next_route(req: NextRequest):
    try:
        return collector.next_item(req.rater_id, req.rater_role, req.rater_experience)
    except ValueError as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(exc))


@router.post("/api/collect/judge")
async def judge_route(req: JudgeRequest):
    try:
        return collector.judge(req.pair_id, req.choice, req.decision_ms)
    except (KeyError, ValueError) as exc:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(exc))
```

### collect_images.py
```
"""Render the six standard views of a transfer function as one grid image,
for the preference-collection page (`collect.py`): the rater judges exactly
what the reward model will later see, not a live 3D viewport.

`_render_views` is the one seam that touches VTK/dataset loading -- tests
stub it out, so `view_grid`'s caching and grid-composition logic is testable
without a real renderer. A labelled volume renders label-scoped with the
current task's unlabelled-shell opacity (`transfer.UNLABELLED_OPACITY`, set by
`goals.use_task_version`), exactly as the viewer shows it.
"""
import base64
import hashlib
import io
import os

import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

import render as render_module
import totalseg
import transfer
import views
from anatomy_layers import default_layers
from datasets import _dataset_version, label_cache_identity, load_dataset

CACHE_DIR = "out/cache/collect_images"
# 2: label-scoped renders with the task's shell opacity, frames cropped
# instead of squeezed into a square (2026-10-19).
CACHE_VERSION = 2
CROP_MARGIN = 1.08          # around the widest horizontal extent the six views can show


def _cache_key(volume_id: str, volume_version: str, params: np.ndarray, size: int,
               columns: int, label_identity: str = "") -> str:
    param_str = ",".join(f"{v:.6f}" for v in np.asarray(params, dtype=np.float64).tolist())
    payload = "|".join([volume_id, volume_version, label_identity, param_str,
                         str(size), str(columns), f"{transfer.UNLABELLED_OPACITY:.3f}",
                         str(CACHE_VERSION)])
    return hashlib.sha256(payload.encode()).hexdigest()


def _cache_path(cache_key: str, cache_dir: str = None) -> str:
    return os.path.join(cache_dir or CACHE_DIR, f"{cache_key}.png")


def _crop_columns(volume, spacing, width: int, height: int) -> tuple:
    """The pixel columns any of the six views can draw the volume in.

    The views turn around the head-to-foot axis with a parallel projection of
    half-height `views.PARALLEL_SCALE_FACTOR` x the largest extent, so the
    widest a volume can appear is its axial diagonal -- the same columns for
    every view and transfer function of a volume, so candidates stay
    comparable pixel for pixel."""
    extent = np.asarray(volume.shape, dtype=np.float64) * np.asarray(spacing, dtype=np.float64)
    px_per_mm = height / (2.0 * views.PARALLEL_SCALE_FACTOR * float(extent.max()))
    half = int(np.ceil(0.5 * float(np.hypot(extent[0], extent[1])) * CROP_MARGIN * px_per_mm))
    centre = width // 2
    return max(0, centre - half), min(width, centre + half)


def _render_views(volume_id: str, params: np.ndarray) -> list:
    """The six standard views of `volume_id` under `params`, one RGB array
    per `views.cameras_for_volume` camera, via `render.render`/`render.grab`,
    cropped to the columns the volume can occupy (`_crop_columns`)."""
    volume, spacing = load_dataset(volume_id, canonical=True)
    labels = totalseg.load_labels(volume_id) if totalseg.is_totalseg(volume_id) and \
        totalseg.has_labels(volume_id) else None
    layers = default_layers() if labels is not None else None
    cameras = views.cameras_for_volume(volume, spacing)
    frames = []
    for camera in cameras:
        win = render_module.render(volume, params, spacing, camera, labels=labels, layers=layers)
        frame = render_module.grab(win)
        left, right = _crop_columns(volume, spacing, frame.shape[1], frame.shape[0])
        frames.append(frame[:, left:right, :3])
    return frames


def _compose_grid(frames: list, size: int, columns: int) -> Image.Image:
    """`frames` as a grid `columns` wide, each cell `size` pixels high and
    as wide as the frame's own aspect ratio allows."""
    rows = -(-len(frames) // columns)  # ceil division
    first = np.asarray(frames[0])
    cell_w = max(1, round(size * first.shape[1] / first.shape[0]))
    grid = Image.new("RGB", (cell_w * columns, size * rows), (0, 0, 0))
    for index, frame in enumerate(frames):
        thumb = Image.fromarray(np.asarray(frame, dtype=np.uint8)).resize((cell_w, size), Image.Resampling.BILINEAR)
        row, col = divmod(index, columns)
        grid.paste(thumb, (col * cell_w, row * size))
    return grid


def view_grid(volume_id: str, params: np.ndarray, size: int = 320, columns: int = 3,
              cache_dir: str = None, label_identity: str = "") -> bytes:
    """PNG bytes of the six standard views of `volume_id` under `params`,
    laid out `columns`-wide. Cached on disk under `<cache_dir>/<sha256 of
    volume version, params and size>.png`; written atomically (`.tmp` then
    `os.replace`) so a reader never sees a partial file."""
    volume_version = _dataset_version(volume_id)
    params = np.asarray(params, dtype=np.float64)
    if not label_identity:
        try:
            label_identity = label_cache_identity(volume_id)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            pass
    key = _cache_key(volume_id, volume_version, params, size, columns, label_identity)
    path = _cache_path(key, cache_dir)
    if os.path.exists(path):
        with open(path, "rb") as f:
            return f.read()

    frames = _render_views(volume_id, params)
    grid = _compose_grid(frames, size, columns)
    buf = io.BytesIO()
    grid.save(buf, format="PNG")
    png_bytes = buf.getvalue()

    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    tmp_path = path + ".tmp"
    with open(tmp_path, "wb") as f:
        f.write(png_bytes)
    os.replace(tmp_path, path)
    return png_bytes


def grid_data_url(volume_id: str, params: np.ndarray, size: int = 320, columns: int = 3,
                  cache_dir: str = None, label_identity: str = "") -> str:
    """`view_grid`'s PNG as a `data:image/png;base64,...` string, for
    embedding directly in a JSON response."""
    png_bytes = view_grid(volume_id, params, size=size, columns=columns,
                          cache_dir=cache_dir, label_identity=label_identity)
    return "data:image/png;base64," + base64.b64encode(png_bytes).decode()
```

### commands.py
```
"""Text -> command dict (two implementations) and command -> params."""
import datetime as _dt
import difflib
import json as _json
import os
import re
import time
from urllib.error import URLError
from urllib.request import Request, urlopen

import numpy as np

import anatomy
import lexicon
from anatomy_layers import default_layers, normalize_layers
from transfer import (
    ANATOMICAL_CENTRES_HU, ANATOMICAL_WIDTHS_HU, CENTER_RANGE, N_PEAKS,
    PARAMS_PER_PEAK, SHOW_ONLY_MAX_WIDTH_HU, TISSUE_BANDS, TISSUE_HU,
    WIDTH_RANGE, _from_range, _from_unit, default_params, peak_internal,
)

# Vocabulary lives in data/lexicon.json (lexicon.py), shared with speech
# recognition and the LLM prompt.
_LEX = lexicon.get()
CLASS_SYNONYMS = {name: list(_LEX["classes"][name]) for name in anatomy.CANONICAL_CLASSES}
_CLASS_LOOKUP = sorted(
    ((phrase, cls) for cls, phrases in CLASS_SYNONYMS.items() for phrase in phrases),
    key=lambda t: -len(t[0]),
)

# fat/air/spongy are retired -- no goal class isolates them any more, and
# silently remapping them to something else would corrupt collected
# preference data. The rule parser rejects them outright.
RETIRED_CLASS_WORDS = {name: list(words) for name, words in _LEX["retired"].items()}
_RETIRED_LOOKUP = sorted(
    ((phrase, name) for name, phrases in RETIRED_CLASS_WORDS.items() for phrase in phrases),
    key=lambda t: -len(t[0]),
)


class RetiredClassError(ValueError):
    """A retired class (fat/air/spongy) was named: refused, never remapped."""


def _check_retired(t: str) -> None:
    for phrase, name in _RETIRED_LOOKUP:
        if re.search(rf"\b{re.escape(phrase)}\b", t):
            raise RetiredClassError(
                f"{name!r} is no longer a supported class -- the supported "
                f"classes are: {', '.join(CLASS_SYNONYMS)}"
            )


def _find_class(text: str):
    for phrase, cls in _CLASS_LOOKUP:
        if phrase in text:
            return cls
    return None


def _split_class_list(text: str) -> list:
    """'bone and lungs' / 'bone, lungs' / 'bone + lungs' -> ['skeleton', 'lungs']."""
    parts = re.split(r"\s*(?:,|\+|\band\b)\s*", text.strip())
    classes = []
    for part in parts:
        cls = _find_class(part)
        if cls and cls not in classes:
            classes.append(cls)
    return classes


_STRENGTH_MODIFIER_WORDS = {"a bit": "slightly", "a lot": "strongly", "much": "strongly"}


def _parse_relative_clause(clause: str):
    """One relative clause -> a single relative command dict, or None if the
    clause isn't one of the recognized relative phrasings."""
    m = re.search(r"\b(increase|decrease)\s+(opacity|width|sharpness|brightness)\s+(?:for|of)\s+([\w ]+)", clause)
    if m:
        direction, attr_word, target_text = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
        attribute = ATTRIBUTE_WORD_ALIASES.get(attr_word, attr_word)
        strength = _strength_in(target_text)
        cls = _find_class(target_text)
        if cls:
            return {"target": cls, "attribute": attribute,
                    "direction": direction, "strength": strength}
        return None

    m = re.search(r"\b(?:(a bit|a lot|much)\s+)?(more|less)\s+([\w ]+)", clause)
    if m:
        modifier, verb, target_text = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
        cls = _find_class(target_text)
        if cls:
            strength = _STRENGTH_MODIFIER_WORDS.get(modifier) or _strength_in(clause)
            direction = "increase" if verb == "more" else "decrease"
            return {"target": cls, "attribute": "opacity",
                    "direction": direction, "strength": strength}
    return None

COMMAND_REFERENCE = [
    {
        "category": "Opacity (relative)",
        "examples": ["increase opacity for skeleton strongly", "decrease opacity for lungs slightly",
                     "more bone", "a bit less soft tissue", "more heart", "more liver",
                     "more kidneys", "more spleen", "more aorta", "hide the lungs",
                     "remove the skeleton"],
        "description": "Nudge a class's visibility up or down by a relative amount. "
                        "\"hide\"/\"remove\" are a decrease at strength \"strongly\".",
    },
    {
        "category": "Opacity (absolute)",
        "examples": ["high opacity vessels", "low opacity for skeleton"],
        "description": "Set a class's visibility to a fixed low/medium/high level.",
    },
    {
        "category": "Show only",
        "examples": ["show only skeleton", "show only skeleton and lungs", "show only heart",
                     "show only vessels", "show only liver", "show only kidneys",
                     "show only spleen", "show me the lungs"],
        "description": "Isolate one or more classes, crushing every other peak to zero. "
                       "Isolation uses approximate HU bands, not anatomical guarantees; "
                       "overlapping tissues can remain visible.",
    },
    {
        "category": "Compound",
        "examples": ["high opacity vessels, low opacity skeleton",
                     "more bone, a bit less soft tissue"],
        "description": "Set several classes' levels, or nudge several classes' opacity, in one command.",
    },
    {
        "category": "Width / sharpness",
        "examples": ["sharpen the skeleton peak", "soften soft tissue", "increase width for lungs"],
        "description": "Adjust how spread out (blended) or narrow (selective) a class's peak is.",
    },
    {
        "category": "Brightness",
        "examples": ["brighten skeleton", "darken lungs", "brighten heart", "brighten liver",
                     "brighten kidneys", "brighten spleen", "low brightness for vessels"],
        "description": "Adjust a class's color brightness.",
    },
    {
        "category": "Center position",
        "examples": ["shift skeleton's center up", "move lungs down", "shift skeleton's position down"],
        "description": "Nudge where in Hounsfield space a class's peak sits, clamped to that class's own band.",
    },
    {
        "category": "Camera",
        "examples": ["rotate left", "tilt down", "zoom in"],
        "description": "Adjust the viewing angle or zoom level. Doesn't change the transfer function.",
    },
    {
        "category": "Reset",
        "examples": ["reset"],
        "description": "Return the transfer function to its default state.",
    },
]

DEFAULT_LLM_MODEL = "qwen2.5:7b"
UTTERANCE_LOG_PATH = "out/utterances.jsonl"
STRENGTH_WORDS = {"slightly": 0.15, "moderately": 0.35, "strongly": 0.6}
NEAR_THRESHOLD_HU = 300.0

DIRECT_VERBS = {
    "sharpen": ("width", "decrease", "moderately"),
    "soften": ("width", "increase", "moderately"),
    "brighten": ("brightness", "increase", "moderately"),
    "darken": ("brightness", "decrease", "moderately"),
    # There is no "hide" direction (see _SYSTEM_PROMPT): hiding a class
    # completely is "decrease" with strength "strongly". The LLM parser
    # already knows this from the prompt; the rule parser had no vocabulary
    # for "hide"/"remove" at all, so it raised on a very natural phrasing
    # instead of falling back to it.
    "hide": ("opacity", "decrease", "strongly"),
    "remove": ("opacity", "decrease", "strongly"),
    "reduce": ("opacity", "decrease", "moderately"),
}

ATTRIBUTE_WORD_ALIASES = {"sharpness": "width"}


def _normalise_text(text: str) -> str:
    t = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text.lower().strip())
    return re.sub(r"[.!?]+$", "", t).strip()


_SLIGHT_RE = lexicon.strength_pattern(_LEX, "slightly")
_STRONG_RE = lexicon.strength_pattern(_LEX, "strongly")


def _strength_in(text: str, default: str = "moderately") -> str:
    """The strength a phrase asks for: an explicit strength word, or a
    modifier ("a bit", "a lot", "extremely"), else `default`."""
    for word in STRENGTH_WORDS:
        if re.search(rf"\b{word}\b", text):
            return word
    if _SLIGHT_RE.search(text):
        return "slightly"
    if _STRONG_RE.search(text):
        return "strongly"
    return default


def _find_classes(text: str) -> list:
    """Every class named in `text`, in order of appearance: whole words
    (plurals allowed), longest phrase first, no overlaps ("pulmonary tissue"
    is lungs, not lungs + soft)."""
    taken, found = [], []
    for phrase, cls in _CLASS_LOOKUP:
        for m in re.finditer(rf"\b{re.escape(phrase)}(?:s|es)?\b", text):
            if any(m.start() < end and start < m.end() for start, end in taken):
                continue
            taken.append((m.start(), m.end()))
            found.append((m.start(), cls))
    classes = []
    for _, cls in sorted(found):
        if cls not in classes:
            classes.append(cls)
    return classes


_SINGLE_WORD_SYNONYMS = {phrase: cls for phrase, cls in _CLASS_LOOKUP if " " not in phrase}


def _fuzzy_classes(text: str) -> list:
    """Classes behind misspelt or misheard words ("langs" -> lungs), for a
    clause that names none exactly."""
    classes = []
    for word in re.findall(r"[a-z]+", text):
        if len(word) < 4:
            continue
        close = difflib.get_close_matches(word, _SINGLE_WORD_SYNONYMS, n=1, cutoff=0.8)
        if close and _SINGLE_WORD_SYNONYMS[close[0]] not in classes:
            classes.append(_SINGLE_WORD_SYNONYMS[close[0]])
    return classes


_RESET_RE = re.compile(r"\b(reset|revert|start over|start again|from scratch|defaults?|original settings)\b"
                       r"|\bput (?:\w+ ){1,3}back\b")
_PRONOUN_RE = re.compile(r"\b(it|them|they|those|these|that|this)\b")
_RELATIVE_CUE_RE = re.compile(r"\b(more|less|increase\w*|decrease\w*|brighte\w*|darke\w*|lighte\w*)\b")
_SHOW_ONLY_RE = re.compile(r"\b(only|isolate|nothing but|exclusively)\b|^just\b|\b(show|display)\b(?! (more|less)\b)")
_BRIGHTNESS_RE = re.compile(r"\b(bright\w*|light(?:en|er)\w*|dark\w*|dim\w*)\b")
_DECREASE_RE = re.compile(
    r"\b(less|decrease\w*|reduc\w*|lower\w*|fade\w*|hide|hiding|remove|get rid of|eliminate|weaken\w*|"
    r"transparent|translucent|see through|dominat\w*|drown\w*|obscur\w*|block\w*|cut back|"
    r"too (?:strong|much|visible|opaque|prominent|dense))\b"
    r"|\b(?:turn|tone|dial)\w*\b.*\bdown\b|\bpull\w*\b.*\bback\b")
_INCREASE_RE = re.compile(
    r"\b(more|increase\w*|add|boost\w*|raise|bring\w*|stand out|emphasi[sz]e\w*|highlight\w*|"
    r"strengthen\w*|pop|punch\w*|enhance\w*|better|can'?t see|cannot see|barely|faint|invisible|"
    r"washed out|missing|visible|opaque|stronger)\b|\b(?:turn|dial)\w*\b.*\bup\b")
_BRIGHTNESS_DOWN_RE = re.compile(r"\b(darken\w*|darker|dim\w*|too bright|decrease\w*|reduc\w*|less|lower\w*)\b")
_BRIGHTNESS_UP_RE = re.compile(r"\b(brighten\w*|brighter|lighten\w*|lighter|too dark|increase\w*|more|raise)\b")


def _free_direction(clause: str, attribute: str):
    if attribute == "brightness":
        if _BRIGHTNESS_DOWN_RE.search(clause):
            return "decrease"
        if _BRIGHTNESS_UP_RE.search(clause):
            return "increase"
        return "decrease" if re.search(r"\b(dark|dim)", clause) else "increase"
    # Decrease cues first: "more transparent" and "less visible" are decreases.
    if _DECREASE_RE.search(clause):
        return "decrease"
    if _INCREASE_RE.search(clause):
        return "increase"
    return None


_NEGATOR_RE = re.compile(r"\b(less|too|not|no longer)\b")


def _parse_free_clause(clause: str, previous_target=None):
    """A clause in everyday words: a class (named, misspelt, or "it"/"them"
    for the previous clause's class) plus a cue for what should happen to it --
    a level ("high spleen", "set the liver to low"), a brightness word, or an
    opacity verb or description ("add", "tone down", "barely visible",
    "dominating the view"). Returns a list of sub-commands, or None."""
    classes = _find_classes(clause) or _fuzzy_classes(clause)
    if not classes:
        if previous_target is None or not _PRONOUN_RE.search(clause):
            return None
        classes = [previous_target]
    attribute = "brightness" if _BRIGHTNESS_RE.search(clause) else "opacity"
    level = re.search(r"\b(low|medium|high)\b", clause)
    if level:
        return [{"target": c, "attribute": attribute, "direction": "set", "level": level.group(1)}
                for c in classes]
    match = lexicon.find_cue_match(clause, _LEX)
    if match and match[0].get("direction") in ("increase", "decrease"):
        cue, start, phrase = match
        direction = cue["direction"]
        # "less transparent", "too dim", "no longer solid" mean the opposite
        # of the cue -- unless the phrase carries its own comparative
        # ("less opaque", "more transparent").
        if _NEGATOR_RE.search(clause[:start]) and not re.search(r"\b(less|more)\b", phrase):
            direction = "increase" if direction == "decrease" else "decrease"
        attribute = cue.get("attribute", attribute)
        strength = _strength_in(clause, cue.get("strength", "moderately"))
        return [{"target": c, "attribute": attribute, "direction": direction, "strength": strength}
                for c in classes]
    direction = _free_direction(clause, attribute)
    if direction is None:
        return None
    default = "strongly" if re.search(r"\b(hide|remove|get rid of|eliminate)\b", clause) else "moderately"
    strength = _strength_in(clause, default)
    return [{"target": c, "attribute": attribute, "direction": direction, "strength": strength}
            for c in classes]


def _cue_direction(t: str):
    cue = lexicon.find_cue(t, _LEX)
    return cue.get("direction") if cue else None


def _parse_show_only(t: str):
    """"show only the heart", "just the kidneys", "isolate the liver", "I only
    want to see the vessels" -> show_only over every class named."""
    if not (_SHOW_ONLY_RE.search(t) or _cue_direction(t) == "show_only") or _RELATIVE_CUE_RE.search(t):
        return None
    classes = _find_classes(t) or _fuzzy_classes(t)
    if not classes:
        return None
    return {"target": classes[0] if len(classes) == 1 else classes,
            "attribute": "opacity", "direction": "show_only", "strength": None}


def _parse_camera(t: str):
    m = re.search(r"\b(?:rotate|turn)\b(?:\s+\w+){0,3}?\s+(left|right)\b", t)
    if m:
        return {"camera": {"action": "rotate", "direction": m.group(1), "strength": _strength_in(t)}}
    m = re.search(r"\btilt\b(?:\s+\w+){0,2}?\s+(up|down)\b", t)
    if m:
        return {"camera": {"action": "tilt", "direction": m.group(1), "strength": _strength_in(t)}}
    m = re.search(r"\bzoom\s+(in|out)\b", t)
    if m:
        return {"camera": {"action": "zoom", "direction": m.group(1), "strength": _strength_in(t)}}
    return None


def _parse_grammar_clause(t: str):
    """The fixed grammar (COMMANDS.md) for one clause; a list of
    sub-commands, or None."""
    m = re.search(r"show\s+(more|less)\s+([\w ]+)", t)
    if m:
        cls = _find_class(m.group(2))
        if cls:
            return [{"target": cls, "attribute": "opacity",
                     "direction": "increase" if m.group(1) == "more" else "decrease",
                     "strength": "moderately"}]

    m = re.search(r"\b(sharpen|soften|brighten|darken|hide|remove|reduce)\b\s+([\w ]+)", t)
    if m:
        verb, target_text = m.group(1), m.group(2)
        attribute, direction, strength = DIRECT_VERBS[verb]
        cls = _find_class(target_text)
        if cls:
            return [{"target": cls, "attribute": attribute, "direction": direction,
                     "strength": _strength_in(target_text, strength)}]

    m = re.search(r"\b(?:shift|move)\s+([\w ]+?)(?:'s)?\s+(?:center|position)?\s*(up|down|higher|lower)\b", t)
    if m:
        cls = _find_class(m.group(1))
        if cls:
            direction = "increase" if m.group(2) in ("up", "higher") else "decrease"
            return [{"target": cls, "attribute": "center", "direction": direction, "strength": "moderately"}]

    # "high opacity vessels", "low opacity for skeleton", "high sharpness bone":
    # an absolute level per class+attribute, not a relative delta.
    subcommands = []
    for lm in re.finditer(r"(low|medium|high)\s+(opacity|width|sharpness|brightness)\s+(?:for\s+)?(?:the\s+)?(\w+)", t):
        cls = _find_class(lm.group(3))
        if cls:
            subcommands.append({"target": cls, "attribute": ATTRIBUTE_WORD_ALIASES.get(lm.group(2), lm.group(2)),
                                "direction": "set", "level": lm.group(1)})
    if subcommands:
        return subcommands

    single = _parse_relative_clause(t)
    return [single] if single else None


_PURPOSE_RE = re.compile(r"\s*\b(so that|so|in order to|because)\b.*$")
_CLAUSE_SPLIT_RE = re.compile(
    r"\s*(?:[,;+]|\band\b|\bbut\b|\bthen\b|\bplus\b|\bwhile\b|\binstead\b(?!\s+of\b))\s*")


_FILLER_RE = re.compile(r"\b(please|again|even|the|a|an|just|okay|ok)\b")


def _is_modifier_only(clause: str) -> bool:
    """A clause with nothing but strength, verb or filler words ("extremely",
    "darken", "a lot please") belongs to its neighbour."""
    rest = clause
    for pattern in (_SLIGHT_RE, _STRONG_RE, _DECREASE_RE, _INCREASE_RE, _BRIGHTNESS_RE, _FILLER_RE):
        rest = pattern.sub(" ", rest)
    return not rest.strip() and not _find_classes(clause)


def _merge_modifier_clauses(clauses: list) -> list:
    if len(clauses) < 2:
        return clauses
    merged, pending = [], []
    for clause in clauses:
        if _is_modifier_only(clause):
            pending.append(clause)
            continue
        merged.append(" ".join(pending + [clause]))
        pending = []
    if pending:
        if not merged:
            return [" ".join(pending)]
        merged[-1] = " ".join([merged[-1]] + pending)
    return merged


def _is_reset(t: str) -> bool:
    return bool(_RESET_RE.search(t)) or _cue_direction(t) == "reset"


def _class_reset(classes: list) -> dict:
    return {"target": classes[0] if len(classes) == 1 else classes,
            "attribute": None, "direction": "reset", "strength": None}


def _is_class_list(clause: str) -> bool:
    """Nothing but class names and filler ("the liver", "lungs")."""
    if not _find_classes(clause):
        return False
    rest = clause
    for phrase, _ in _CLASS_LOOKUP:
        rest = re.sub(rf"\b{re.escape(phrase)}(?:s|es)?\b", " ", rest)
    return not _FILLER_RE.sub(" ", rest).strip()


def _split_clauses(t: str) -> list:
    return _merge_modifier_clauses([c for c in _CLAUSE_SPLIT_RE.split(t) if c])


def parse_command_rule(text: str) -> dict:
    """Text -> command, without a language model.

    The fixed grammar in COMMANDS.md parses exactly as before; everyday
    phrasings ("add bones", "set the spleen to high", "the aorta is
    dominating the view, pull it back", "darken langs extremely") go through
    `_parse_free_clause`. A sentence is split into clauses (commas, "and",
    "but"); every clause must parse, or the whole command is refused rather
    than silently dropping part of what was said."""
    t = _normalise_text(text)

    _check_retired(t)

    # A reset word resets everything, unless a clause pairs it with classes
    # ("revert the skeleton"): then that clause resets those classes and the
    # other clauses parse as usual ("..., instead darken the lungs").
    reset_clauses = None
    if _is_reset(t):
        reset_clauses = _split_clauses(t)
        if not any(_is_reset(c) and _find_classes(c) for c in reset_clauses):
            return {"target": None, "attribute": None, "direction": "reset", "strength": None}

    if reset_clauses is not None:
        clauses = reset_clauses
    else:
        camera = _parse_camera(t)
        if camera:
            return camera

        t = _PURPOSE_RE.sub("", t) or t
        show_only = _parse_show_only(t)
        if show_only:
            return show_only

        clauses = _split_clauses(t)
    subcommands, previous = [], None
    for clause in clauses:
        if _is_reset(clause) and _find_classes(clause):
            parsed = [_class_reset(_find_classes(clause))]
        elif reset_clauses is not None and _parse_show_only(clause):
            parsed = [_parse_show_only(clause)]
        else:
            parsed = _parse_grammar_clause(clause) or _parse_free_clause(clause, previous)
        if not parsed and subcommands and subcommands[-1]["direction"] == "reset" and _is_class_list(clause):
            # "reset the lungs and the liver": a bare class list continues the reset.
            last = subcommands[-1]["target"]
            last = last if isinstance(last, list) else [last]
            subcommands[-1] = _class_reset(last + [c for c in _find_classes(clause) if c not in last])
            continue
        if not parsed:
            if len(clauses) > 1:
                raise ValueError(f"could not parse clause {clause!r} in compound command: {text!r}")
            raise ValueError(f"rule parser cannot parse: {text!r}")
        subcommands.extend(parsed)
        previous = parsed[-1]["target"]
        if isinstance(previous, list):
            previous = previous[-1]
    # "the heart is too strong, tone it down" says one thing twice.
    unique = []
    for sub in subcommands:
        if not any((u["target"], u["attribute"], u["direction"]) == (sub["target"], sub["attribute"], sub["direction"])
                   for u in unique):
            unique.append(sub)
    return unique[0] if len(unique) == 1 else {"compound": unique}


def parse_command_with_meta(text: str, parser: str = "rule", model: str = DEFAULT_LLM_MODEL):
    """`parse_command`, plus which parser actually produced the command.

    `parse_command_llm` falls back to the rule parser whenever Ollama is
    unreachable or answers with an invalid schema, and only says so on the
    server's stdout -- so a viewer with the llm toggle on could show a rule
    parse and call it an LLM one. Returns `(cmd, meta)` with meta =
    {"parser_requested", "parser_used", "parser_fallback"}, where
    `parser_fallback` is the reason string when llm was asked for and rule
    answered, and None otherwise. Hybrid adds "normalized", "uncovered",
    "parse_ms", "understood" and "parse_warning" (see parse_command_hybrid)."""
    meta = {"parser_requested": parser, "parser_used": parser, "parser_fallback": None}
    if parser == "hybrid":
        cmd, hmeta = parse_command_hybrid(text, model=model)
        return cmd, {"parser_requested": "hybrid", "parser_used": hmeta["path"],
                     "parser_fallback": hmeta["fallback"], "normalized": hmeta["normalized"],
                     "uncovered": hmeta["uncovered"], "parse_ms": hmeta["parse_ms"],
                     "understood": hmeta["understood"], "parse_warning": hmeta["warning"]}
    if parser != "llm":
        return parse_command_rule(text), meta

    reasons = []
    cmd = parse_command_llm(text, model=model, _on_fallback=reasons.append)
    if reasons:
        meta["parser_used"] = "rule"
        meta["parser_fallback"] = reasons[0]
    return cmd, meta


def _peak_center_hu(params: np.ndarray, i: int) -> float:
    return peak_internal(params, i)["center"]


# Keep retired direct-application names working, while anatomical targets use
# the same centres and widths as their transfer peaks.
CLASS_HU = {**TISSUE_HU, **ANATOMICAL_CENTRES_HU}
CLASS_BANDS = {**TISSUE_BANDS, **{
    name: (centre - width, centre + width)
    for name, centre in ANATOMICAL_CENTRES_HU.items()
    for width in (ANATOMICAL_WIDTHS_HU[name],)
}}

_LEGACY_APPLY_PEAK_INDEX = {"bone": 3, "spongy": 2, "fat": 1, "air": 0}


def _class_peak_index(params: np.ndarray, tissue: str):
    """The peak `_find_or_create_peak` would change for `tissue`, or None
    when no peak is near enough (it would reseed one)."""
    if tissue in _LEGACY_APPLY_PEAK_INDEX:
        return _LEGACY_APPLY_PEAK_INDEX[tissue]
    distances = [abs(_peak_center_hu(params, i) - CLASS_HU[tissue]) for i in range(N_PEAKS)]
    idx = int(np.argmin(distances))
    return idx if distances[idx] <= NEAR_THRESHOLD_HU else None


def _find_or_create_peak(params: np.ndarray, tissue: str):
    """Nearest peak to the tissue's HU; reseed the weakest peak if none is near."""
    target_hu = CLASS_HU[tissue]
    idx = _class_peak_index(params, tissue)
    params = params.copy()
    if idx is None:
        heights = [peak_internal(params, i)["height"] for i in range(N_PEAKS)]
        idx = int(np.argmin(heights))
        base = idx * PARAMS_PER_PEAK
        params[base + 0] = _from_range(target_hu, *CENTER_RANGE)
        params[base + 1] = _from_range(80.0, *WIDTH_RANGE)
        params[base + 2] = _from_unit(0.05)
    return params, idx


LEVEL_WORDS = {"low": 0.15, "medium": 0.5, "high": 0.85}

ATTRIBUTE_PARAM_INDEX = {"opacity": 2, "width": 1, "brightness": (3, 4, 5), "center": 0}


def _asymptotic_step(current_ext: float, direction: str, delta: float) -> float:
    # Move a fraction of the remaining headroom to the +1/-1 bound, not a
    # fixed absolute amount -- generalizes the height-only fix (a fixed step
    # saturated almost any starting value in one command) to any attribute
    # stored in the same normalized external [-1, 1] encoding.
    if direction == "increase":
        return current_ext + delta * (1.0 - current_ext)
    return current_ext - delta * (current_ext + 1.0)


def _center_band_clip(ext_value: float, tissue: str) -> float:
    # A center shift must stay within the target tissue's own HU band --
    # otherwise repeated shifts could walk a peak out of its own tissue
    # entirely and into a neighboring one's territory, silently.
    lo_hu, hi_hu = CLASS_BANDS[tissue]
    lo_ext = _from_range(lo_hu, *CENTER_RANGE)
    hi_ext = _from_range(hi_hu, *CENTER_RANGE)
    return float(np.clip(ext_value, lo_ext, hi_ext))


def apply_command(cmd: dict, params: np.ndarray, reset_params=None) -> np.ndarray:
    """`cmd` applied to `params`. "reset" returns `reset_params` (the viewer's
    start TF for its task version), `default_params()` when not given; a
    "reset" with a class target restores only that class's peak from it."""
    if "compound" in cmd:
        # Multiple tissues set to different absolute levels in one utterance
        # ("high opacity spongy, low opacity bone") -- fold each sub-command
        # through this same function in sequence. Not a search target: an
        # absolute multi-tissue assignment isn't something to hill-climb.
        for sub in cmd["compound"]:
            params = apply_command(sub, params, reset_params)
        return params

    params = params.copy()

    if cmd["direction"] == "reset":
        reference = default_params() if reset_params is None else np.array(reset_params, dtype=np.float64)
        if cmd.get("target") is None:
            return reference
        # A class reset ("revert the skeleton") restores that class's peak and
        # nothing else. The class's slot can differ between the two TFs (a
        # command reseeded a peak for it, or the reset TF has none near), so
        # every slot holding the class in either one goes back.
        targets = cmd["target"] if isinstance(cmd["target"], list) else [cmd["target"]]
        for tissue in targets:
            for idx in {_class_peak_index(params, tissue), _class_peak_index(reference, tissue)} - {None}:
                peak = slice(idx * PARAMS_PER_PEAK, (idx + 1) * PARAMS_PER_PEAK)
                params[peak] = reference[peak]
        return params

    if cmd["direction"] == "show_only":
        targets = cmd["target"] if isinstance(cmd["target"], list) else [cmd["target"]]
        target_idxs = set()
        for tissue in targets:
            params, idx = _find_or_create_peak(params, tissue)
            target_idxs.add(idx)
        for idx in target_idxs:
            # A wide peak still has real opacity reaching into a neighbour's
            # HU range, so boosting it alone lights up other tissue too --
            # cap (never widen) so an already-narrow peak is untouched.
            base = idx * PARAMS_PER_PEAK
            current_width = peak_internal(params, idx)["width"]
            shown_width = SHOW_ONLY_MAX_WIDTH_HU if tissue == "bone" else min(
                current_width, SHOW_ONLY_MAX_WIDTH_HU)
            params[base + 1] = _from_range(shown_width, *WIDTH_RANGE)
        for i in range(N_PEAKS):
            b = i * PARAMS_PER_PEAK
            params[b + 2] = _from_unit(0.7 if i in target_idxs else 0.0)
        return params

    params, idx = _find_or_create_peak(params, cmd["target"])
    base = idx * PARAMS_PER_PEAK
    indices = ATTRIBUTE_PARAM_INDEX[cmd["attribute"]]
    if isinstance(indices, int):
        indices = (indices,)

    if cmd["direction"] == "set":
        if cmd["attribute"] == "center":
            raise ValueError("center has no absolute 'set' -- only increase/decrease")
        new_ext = _from_unit(LEVEL_WORDS[cmd["level"]])
        for offset in indices:
            params[base + offset] = new_ext
        return params

    delta = STRENGTH_WORDS[cmd["strength"] or "moderately"]
    for offset in indices:
        new_ext = _asymptotic_step(float(params[base + offset]), cmd["direction"], delta)
        if cmd["attribute"] == "center":
            new_ext = _center_band_clip(new_ext, cmd["target"])
        params[base + offset] = float(np.clip(new_ext, -1.0, 1.0))
    return params


def apply_command_state(cmd: dict, params: np.ndarray, layers: dict) -> tuple[np.ndarray, dict]:
    """Apply a command to HU peaks and anatomical layer state independently."""
    layer_command = cmd.get("layer_action")
    if cmd.get("attribute") != "layers" and layer_command is None:
        return apply_command(cmd, params), normalize_layers(layers)
    result = normalize_layers(layers)
    action = layer_command["action"] if layer_command else cmd["action"]
    targets = set(layer_command["targets"] if layer_command else cmd["targets"])
    if action == "restore":
        result = default_layers()
    elif action == "show_only":
        for name, layer in result.items():
            layer["opacity"] = 1.0 if name in targets else 0.0
    elif action == "hide":
        for name in targets:
            result[name]["opacity"] = 0.0
    elif action == "show":
        for name in targets:
            result[name]["opacity"] = 1.0
    else:
        raise ValueError(f"unknown layer action: {action}")
    return np.asarray(params, dtype=np.float64).copy(), result


OLLAMA_HOST = os.environ.get("OLLAMA_HOST", "http://localhost:11434")
VALID_DIRECTIONS = {"increase", "decrease", "show_only", "reset"}
VALID_STRENGTHS = set(STRENGTH_WORDS) | {None}
VALID_LEVELS = set(LEVEL_WORDS)
VALID_CAMERA_ACTIONS = {
    "rotate": {"left", "right"},
    "tilt": {"up", "down"},
    "zoom": {"in", "out"},
}
VALID_CAMERA_STRENGTHS = set(STRENGTH_WORDS)  # camera strength has no null case, unlike top-level strength (reset/show_only)
LLM_FORMAT = "schema"  # "schema": constrained decoding to command_schema(); "json": any JSON
THINKING_MODEL_PREFIXES = ("qwen3", "deepseek-r1", "magistral", "gpt-oss")


def command_schema() -> dict:
    """JSON schema of every answer shape, for Ollama's constrained decoding.
    Validation after decoding stays (_validate_cmd): the schema only keeps
```
